**Book 26:** *آرٹ اینڈ ڈرائنگ 7 (Art and Drawing 7)* (student book, Urdu with English lesson titles) — Pakistan, Punjab Curriculum and Textbook Board, Lahore; year 2018 (first edition, February 2018 printing shown on contents page); no ISBN printed, Class 7, 84 PDF pp. STUDENT. Verdict: COMPLETE (cover verified against the record title; 8,109,694 bytes, matches the record exactly). Read from the recorded source_url (Google Drive via freebooks.pk) after grab.py tree returned HTTP 404 — byte size matches the record exactly, so the book is read, but the pushed copy is a PHANTOM PUSH (library debt). The book is a scan with no usable text layer; it was read visually, page by page, from 100-DPI contact sheets. Downloaded copy staged under the push-don't-delete rule at the phase3-staging grade-07 record path (books.json record already exists there). Excluded per standing rules: the Glossary (فرہنگ, printed pp. 80–83) — inventory-only, named here, not stocked. Pakistan's own way: a drawing-first book — every lesson is a pictured construction: set the centre line and eye level, build the object from ellipses and boxes in numbered steps, then finish in colour; its own traditions close the book: Islamic calligraphy written freehand inside geometric frames, a mosque built up step by step and modelled in wheat straw, and abri — marbled paper made with oil colour on water. Read in full in the 2026-11-16 Art expansion read. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/pakistan-placements.md.

**Book 28:** *التربية الفنية — الصف الرابع (Art Education — Grade Four), Primary Stage (المرحلة الابتدائية)* (student book, Arabic) — Sudan, National Center for Curricula and Educational Research (المركز القومي للمناهج والبحث التربوي), Bakht Al-Ruda — Republic of Sudan, Ministry of Education; year 2021; no ISBN printed, Grade 4, 52 PDF pp. STUDENT. Verdict: COMPLETE — read in full, page by page: Drawing field (4 lessons), Design & Decoration field (5 lessons), Forming & Composition field (5 lessons) and the closing picture-models appendix. Sudan's own way: the book teaches in three fields — Drawing, Design & Decoration, Forming & Composition — anchored in the pupil's Sudan: Meroe's pyramids for light and shadow, the Jirtig tray and woven birsh mats for heritage, Nile-valley and Nubian pottery ornament for clay, and palm-frond and straw weaving for the loom. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/sudan-placements.md.

**Book 51:** *Recorriendo el Arte 2 — RECORRIENDO EL ARTE — Educación Artística — 2do AÑO (Colección Bicentenario)* (student book, Spanish) — Venezuela, Ministerio del Poder Popular para la Educación, República Bolivariana de Venezuela; third edition, April 2014; ISBN 978-980-218-331-9 (Depósito Legal lf51620123701319, as printed). Series coordination Ángela Beatriz Calzadilla; same author team as Book 1. Grade 8 (2do año, second year of secondary; student/teacher: STUDENT). PDF pages: 132 (printed pp. 12–255; the fetched PDF is 13,377,588 bytes; cover verified — blue cover with pre-Columbian artefacts). Verdict: COMPLETE — read in full as extracted text (text PDF), cover verified. Named, not placed: the closing teacher-facing essay “Conceptos básicos para la conducción estratégica del aprendizaje artístico” (printed pp. 247–250) — pedagogical guidance for teachers; its core teaching about sensitive knowledge is placed once, as filed; the bibliography (printed pp. 251–255) — inventory only; end-of-chapter suggested activities (their skills folded into the lines they practise). Venezuela's own way: Book 2 walks the whole history of art, starting at home — Venezuela's original peoples first — and returning to Venezuela for the colonial, republican, 20th-century and contemporary chapters. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/venezuela-placements.md.

**Book 52:** *Educação Manual e Plástica — 1.ª Classe* (student book, Portuguese) — Angola, Editora Popular (Ministério da Educação); year 2021; ISBN 978-989-762-287-8. Grade 1 (student/teacher: STUDENT). PDF pages: 45 (cover/title page verified against the record title). Verdict: COMPLETE. Angola's own way: every lesson is a pictured step-by-step making sequence (materials first, then numbered picture steps) — drawing, painting, clay modelling and paper construction taught entirely by doing. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/angola-placements.md.

**Book 53:** *Educação Manual e Plástica — 3.ª Classe, Manual do Aluno* (student book, Portuguese) — Angola, Progresso Editora (Ministério da Educação); year 2018; no ISBN printed (registered at Biblioteca Nacional de Angola no. 4290/07). Grade 3 (student/teacher: STUDENT). PDF pages: 57 (cover/title page verified against the record title). Verdict: COMPLETE (the colour-theory spread the printing chapter points to, printed pp. 18–19, is a divider/blank spread in this PDF; the complementary-pair teaching is placed from §2.2-II itself). Angola's own way: Angolan and African art carries the appreciation content — Cokwe heritage pieces (O Pensador, Mwana Pwo), Benguela dolls, Etona's wood sculpture and Neves e Sousa's painting — and the coil technique is taught by building a sounding clay wind instrument. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/angola-placements.md.

**Book 54:** *Educação Manual e Plástica — Manual da 4.ª Classe* (student book, Portuguese) — Angola, Editora Popular (Ministério da Educação); year 2021; ISBN 978-989-762-263-2. Grade 4 (student/teacher: STUDENT). PDF pages: 53 (cover/title page verified against the record title). Verdict: COMPLETE. Angola's own way: the book teaches children to judge their own work before others do — appreciation and self-improvement (auto-superação), with Angolan basketball player Miguel Lutonda as the example and a painting of Queen Njinga Mbande among the works appreciated. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/angola-placements.md.

**Book 55:** *Educação Manual e Plástica — Manual da 5.ª Classe* (student book, Portuguese) — Angola, Editora das Letras (Ministério da Educação); year 2021 (2nd edition); ISBN 978-989-762-264-9. Grade 5 (student/teacher: STUDENT). PDF pages: 65 (cover/title page verified against the record title). Verdict: COMPLETE. Angola's own way: perspective and colour are taught through Angola itself — the National Assembly building, the Largo da Independência monument and Luanda's Marginal as depth examples, baobabs (embondeiros) in changing light, and Angolan paintings ("As Zungueiras", Guilherme Mampuya's Luanda) beside Rembrandt for chiaroscuro. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/angola-placements.md.

| Sub lesson 7 — Drawing texture (Grade 5): texture felt by touch and sight — smooth: glass, cotton, soft wool; rough: bark, brick wall, sacking; in drawing, roughness by cross-hatching lines or repeated dots, smoothness by smudging or leaving untouched (Fig. 5) | Book 2, printed pp. 6–7 |
| Sub lesson 2 (teacher guide — evidence) — Collage from local materials + design + texture (Grade 4 TG): making collage (ኮላዥ) from materials found in the locality; design (ንድፍ) — direct design and conceptual design (የቀጥታ/ሀሳባዊ ንድፍ); the collage steps; form and surface — smoothness and roughness (ልስላሴና ሻካራነት) by touch and sight | Book 6, §2.4, printed pp. 40–46 |
| Sub lesson 8 — Mosaic, the ancient way (Grade 6): temples'/palaces' floors and walls decorated with coloured stones and pottery fragments; mosaic defined; paper mosaic — glue (mucilage, cola, local paste), tools, steps; Fig. 53 an example paper-mosaic work | Book 7, §4.1.2, printed pp. 87–88 |

| Romania — Portfolio: Cheerful Butterflies — cut butterflies from a magazine, fold each one's wings upward, glue only the body (never the wings) to a folded card so the butterflies seem to fly | Book 13, printed p. 11 |
| Romania — Forms from Mixed Paper: the art of paper mixture (papier-mâché), much loved in China, Japan, India and Mexico — porous paper (newspaper or kitchen paper) with liquid glue, modelled as easily as clay into fruit, animals, jewellery and decorations; the pulp recipe — tear the paper small, soak in hot water 24 hours, mix well by hand to a paste, add glue, flour, salt and a little oil gradually | Book 13, printed p. 16 |
| Romania — Model of a Volcano: build a volcano — fix a plastic bottle on a polystyrene plate, model the pulp over it from base to tip in a cone, groove the top with a pencil, let it dry (cracks make it look more like a volcano), paint with brown tempera and sprinkle sand while wet | Book 13, printed p. 17 |
| Romania — Portfolio: make a string of beads from paper mixture and paint them in your favourite colours | Book 13, printed p. 17 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 14, Paper Lantern — fold paper in half, crease, cut lines through both layers, open, join the two sides with glue, attach strings and hang the lantern | Book 15, printed p. 14 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 15, Paper Doilies — fold coloured paper in half, into quarters, then diagonally; make a series of cuts along the edge and unfold a patterned doily | Book 15, printed p. 15 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 16, Paper Bird — trace around your feet and hands on coloured papers, cut them out, glue the handprints as feathers and the footprints as the body, add a triangular beak, paper eyes and legs | Book 15, printed p. 16 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 18, Paper Photo Frame — draw and cut out flowers and leaves from coloured papers, then frame the page with thin pasted stripes of colour paper | Book 15, printed p. 18 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 27, Pencil Shaving — draw a pineapple's outline and fill it by pasting pencil shavings inside it, with magazine-paper leaves; then make other artworks from pencil shavings | Book 15, printed p. 27 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 28, A Collage of Match Sticks — the teacher demonstrates a star built from match sticks; children make other images from match sticks | Book 15, printed p. 28 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 29, 3D Collage — look at examples of 3D art, discuss what could be made from collected used materials (bottles, straws, plastic, pebbles, wires), build 3D art in groups, and present it: talk about your artwork | Book 15, printed p. 29 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 15, Collage (Paper — Individual) — cut magazines and newspapers into small pieces and paste them onto the outline of a man or woman you have drawn | Book 16, printed p. 15 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 16, Collage (Mixed Media — Group) — in small groups, collect locally available materials, design the artwork in the workbook first, then carry it out together | Book 16, printed p. 16 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 17, Collage (Mixed Media — Whole Class) — the whole class collects materials from in and around the school campus and creates one art work on fixed chart paper | Book 16, printed p. 17 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 18, Paper Frame — make a paper photo frame from small rectangular papers, following the pictured steps | Book 16, printed p. 18 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 19, Paper Flower — fold eight equal square papers through the same sequence of folds (corners to the centre, turn, fold again, fold in half), glue the specified areas and join all eight into a paper flower | Book 16, printed p. 19 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 20, Paper Bird — cut a square, fold it diagonally, fold a centre line, cut as shown, fold both layers up, fold the left corners in to make the beak, fold the bottom up, and draw the eyes | Book 16, printed p. 20 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 21, Paper Balloon — make a paper balloon following the pictured folding steps, then draw the balloon you made | Book 16, printed p. 21 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 22, Paper Plate — make a paper plate following the pictured steps, then draw the plate you made | Book 16, printed p. 22 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 23, Pop-Up Cards — make a pop-up card from colour paper with scissors, glue and crayons, following the teacher's demonstration | Book 16, printed p. 23 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 24, Paper Box — make a paper box along with the teacher's demonstration from the pictures | Book 16, printed p. 24 |
| Bhutan — Lesson 25, Paper Star — make a paper star with paper, glue and scissors, following the demonstration | Book 16, printed p. 25 |

Tseden-Ish), editor С.Нямсүрэн; ISBN 978-99973-69-53-6. Grade 1 (student book: STUDENT). Read as the ministry flipbook's 78 page images = cover + printed pp. 1–76 + back cover; every printed page read. Verdict: COMPLETE. Combined Visual Art & Technology volume: Part II (printed pp. 44–74) is the design-and-making strand — paper, leaf and clay construction, including simple models — placed as art-making; no electronics, machines or other non-art technology content exists in the volume. Exclusions named, not placed: Foreword (printed p. 5); the two poems and the song are placed by name and theme only — no verse text transcribed; Bibliography (printed p. 75 — inventory only). No PDF exists in the repo for this book — the library record is a PHANTOM PUSH (the repo holds no copy, in the tree or in any release); the book was read from the record's own recorded source, the Ministry's econtent.edu.mn flipbook edition, page-image by page-image — the same ministry book, verified by cover, imprint and ISBN against the record. The phantom-push debt stands for the library. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/mongolia-placements.md.

**Book 96:** *Կերպարվեստ 4 (Fine Arts 4)* (student book, Armenian) — Armenia, Zangak, Yerevan; year not printed in the extracted front matter; ISBN not printed in the extracted front matter. Grade: 4 (student/teacher: STUDENT). PDF pages: 96 (cover verified against the record title; byte size 178,089,113 matches the release index exactly; fetched via the recorded ministry source_url with the chunked Range downloader after plain downloads stalled). Verdict: PARTIAL — the book is image-only (no text layer); read visually on contact sheets: the materials/watercolour opening, the mosaic project, the still-life chapter, the old-and-computer-art unit and the complete contents (printed p. 95) in full, the rest sampled. The closing glossary (printed pp. 93-94) is inventory only — named, not stocked, per the standing exclusion. Authors: Marina Petrosyan, Marine Manukyan. Contents map: Methods — materials and means; watercolour; wet technique; dry technique. Elements of fine art and design principles — material and surface; texture; pattern; mosaic. Innovation and imagination — still life; landscape; animal painting; imaginary painting. Presentation — ceramics and exhibition. Workshop. Connections — art, technologies and knowledge: old or computer art. Art history — the animal, portrait and seascape genres. The role and significance of art. Armenia's own way: Saryan's watercolours and still lifes are the working examples, and the "old art" the child compares with computer art includes Urartu bronzes and the Erebuni wall paintings. Read in the 2026-11-16 Art expansion read. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/armenia-placements.md.

Authors: Tatevik Shakhkulyan, Ara Hakobyan, Anush Aslibekyan, Siranuysh Galstyan. The integrated grade-7 course: what art is for, a journey through world art, the elements of music, how theatre and cinema are born, and a long Armenian second half — ashugh art, national song and dance, ritual. Armenia's own way: half the journey is home ground — the Armenian golden age and middle ages get their own "visits", and the book ends inside Armenian ritual: wedding, Barekendan, Hambardzum songs, Vardavar. **Book 64:** Title as printed: هنر رسامی — صنف چهارم (Art of Drawing — Grade Four) Grade: Grade 4 (student/teacher: STUDENT — with a built-in teacher's-guide section, Part 2; noted, not placed separately) Publisher: Ministry of Education, Islamic Republic of Afghanistan; printed 1397 SH (2018/19); no ISBN printed PDF pages: 74 (cover verified visually against the record title; fetched from GitHub release mid-size-books-7, asset grade-04-gov-afghanistan-3251f7822b.pdf, 30,757,525 bytes — secured as a release asset, not in the repo tree) Verdict: COMPLETE Tally: 30 placements — 29 to existing lessons, 1 NEW LESSON NEEDED Afghanistan's own way: Grade 4 drawing becomes real drawing study — outline lines (خطوط بیرونی) first, then rendering (پرداز) with a soft pencil so light and shade (سایه و روشن) stand out; heads are studied by their structure (elephant, ram, horse, birds); colour work adds the two neutral colours, black and white, whose mixture is steel grey (فولادی); and invention lessons run through the year — finger pictures finished with lines, designs from leaves and flowers, shapes blown from colour, and drawing from the mind's eye. Not placed: the Pashto national anthem front matter and Part 2, the built-in teaching guide for the same lessons. **Book 67:** Title as printed: هنر — صنف ۷ (Art — Grade 7; the cover prints "ART" in Latin letters under the Dari title) Grade: Grade 7 (student/teacher: STUDENT) Publisher: Ministry of Education, Islamic Republic of Afghanistan; printed 1399 SH (2020); no ISBN printed PDF pages: 58 (cover verified visually against the record title; fetched from GitHub release mid-size-books-7, asset grade-07-gov-afghanistan-b128b1e7a9.pdf, 10,972,698 bytes — secured as a release asset, not in the repo tree) Verdict: COMPLETE Tally: 24 placements — 15 to existing lessons, 9 NEW LESSON NEEDED Afghanistan's own way: at secondary school, drawing and calligraphy merge into one subject, Art (هنر), and it becomes a real discipline — the elements (line, shape, colour, texture) studied one by one, clay and wire sculpture, and a full art-history spine: Afghanistan's own art from the 100,000-year-old stone tools of the Nawur plain and the cave lines of Aq Kupruk and Hazar Sum, through Mundigak and Gandhara, then the world's art (Mesopotamia, Iran, Egypt, India, China, Japan, Greece, Rome, Christian art), closing with the founders of modern Afghan art and the National Museum that guards it all. **Book 142:** *Mĩ thuật 6 (student book, Vietnamese)* — Title as printed: Mĩ thuật 6 (Kết nối tri thức với cuộc sống) Grade: 6 (student/teacher: STUDENT) Publisher: Nhà xuất bản Giáo dục Việt Nam; year 2021 (5th reprint); ISBN 978-604-0-25150-3 PDF pages: 71 (cover/title page verified against the record title) Verdict: COMPLETE Vietnam's own way: secondary art is organised as eight themes of two lessons each — a forming lesson and a design lesson — and art history enters in earnest: prehistoric and ancient art, world and Vietnamese side by side. | Vietnam — Lesson 4 "Designing a souvenir" — a house-shaped souvenir step by step — cut and fold the card into the house frame, cut and fold the shingled roof, draw and cut the little interior decorations, and glue all together; and what industrial design is — designing products beautifully is part of industrial design, whose works are close to life, from televisions, fridges and phones to medical devices and machinery; the field arose in the 19th century as industrial production of consumer goods grew (student book) | Book 142, printed p. 19 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 5 — a school-activity artwork in paper-pulp relief — sketch the picture; soak tissue paper in water; mix colour into the soaked paper; prepare several colour mixtures; then build the coloured pulp onto the shapes of the drawing (đắp nổi) (student book) | Book 142, printed p. 24 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 6 "Designing toys" — toy design belongs to applied art — shaping, making toys and assembling models from materials, with safety rules when the toy is used; designing school-activity toys — a rotating lantern (đèn kéo quân) and a star lantern (đèn ông sao) — and building a table-football toy: cut a paper box into the pitch, decorate the little stadium, draw two players on card and fix them to wooden skewers (student book) | Book 142, printed p. 27 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 8 — imitating a Vietnamese prehistoric heritage — rebuilding a stone axe from crumpled paper, modelling clay spread on the paper blade, clay on a wooden stick for the haft, and string binding; and decorating the study corner with images from Đồng Nội cave — a clock and a school-supplies box carrying the carved faces (student book) | Book 142, printed p. 37 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 10 "Designing a greeting card" — using folk-game images in a card — fold the card and cut two slits to make the pop-up step, decorate the inside, sketch the game figures, colour and cut them out, and glue them onto the fold; and what graphic design is — a field of applied art whose common products are logo designs, leaflets, greeting cards, packaging, product labels, books, newspapers and magazines (student book) | Book 142, printed p. 42 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 14 "Designing a timetable" — designing your own daily timetable with images and symbols — a wheel of stylised clock characters for the day (going to sleep, waking, exercise, breakfast, school, coming home, tidying the house, helping parents cook, dinner, entertainment, bed) and morning/evening panels in modelling clay; using life's images — postures, activities, figures — makes artworks close and familiar to the viewer's feelings (student book) | Book 142, printed p. 59 |

| Vietnam — Lesson 6 "Designing a birthday gift from available materials" — birthday-gift products from what is at hand — a hydroponic planting box, a set of digital devices from card, a glass-bottle souvenir with a landscape inside; available materials such as plastic boxes, card and glass bottles, once processed, are used to make artworks beautiful both in shape and in the material's own colour, and also help keep the environment green, clean and beautiful; a swan-shaped plant pot from a plastic canister, step by step; and a birthday gift from card with clay relief; birthday gifts can be created from card boxes, plastic tins and bottles, and when making products from such materials one must mind safety in the cutting, joining and decorating operations (student book) | Book 144, printed p. 25 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 8 — making a box for study supplies and decorating its lid in the Expressionist school's style — weaving the box's body from strips, fixing the strips with grass cord, weaving the cord through the whole body and finishing the box; then, after Karl Schmidt-Rottluff's woodcut "Three at the Table" (Ba người trên bàn, Drei am Tisch, 1914): sketch the lines and build the composition, use the way of forming the faces in that painting and draw the image on the lid, paint the colour into the image, and finish (student book) | Book 144, printed p. 34 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 10 "The art of paper-cutting in decoration" — applied artworks using the hand paper-cutting art — a hand-cut paper image decorating a table lamp, and the cut-paper Tortoise card (Thiệp trổ giấy Quy) in the card set "Long, Ly, Quy, Phượng" (Dragon, Unicorn, Tortoise, Phoenix) by Nguyễn Đăng Chế; and making a decorative lamp using hand paper-cutting, step by step — make the lamp's frame from available materials (a carton box, bamboo sticks, wire…), use an image of life's beauty to decorate by the paper-cutting method, stick the decorative image in the lamp's centre position, and adjust the details and finish (student book) | Book 144, printed p. 42 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 11 — designing a public transport vehicle from the drawing to a model in available materials — a boat: show the idea in a design drawing, choose the materials fitting the product from the design drawing, work the materials into each part of the product, and assemble the parts and finish the boat (student book) | Book 144, printed p. 47 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 14 — designing a toy from the drawing to a model in available materials — draw a simple design showing the idea and the materials used, use available materials to shape the toy following the idea in the drawing, make the details and the parts that create the toy box's movement, and assemble the parts and finish; and the marks of a modern design product: aesthetic quality — shown in the shape, mass, colour and material and their harmonious combination; function — applied design always serves a specific purpose, shown in the effectiveness and quality of use, serving life's needs; economy — shown in the ability to be made in favourable conditions, meeting the needs of many people, with a low production cost and high use value; and popularity — the product can be mass-produced, fitting many different users (student book) | Book 144, printed p. 60 |

| Vietnam — Lesson 4 "Designing stands for technology devices" — some styles of technology-device stand — a phone stand made from chain links and screws, a phone stand made from plastic, a tablet stand made from wood and a tablet stand made from plastic; and design drawings of stands (a phone stand combined with a pen box, made from stiff card material; a tablet stand made from mica material); in product design one needs to attend to three basic elements: the idea, the style and the colour, and designing the style of a technology-device stand needs attention to the structure of the stand and the size of the technology device, so as to be firm as well as convenient when used (student book) | Book 145, printed p. 17 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 4 — designing a phone stand from available materials (copper wire), step by step — draw a sketch showing the idea, use pliers to bend the copper wire to shape the product, fix the product's style with the copper wire and use string to decorate, and finish; the basic elements of the product-design field, such as functionality, aesthetic quality, and the combination between novelty and convenience, need to be understood and shown concretely in the designed product, so as to be able to meet the use needs of the age while still keeping the design's own mark (student book) | Book 145, printed p. 19 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 12 — using the forming beauty of the water-puppet figures to show a 3D art product, step by step — sketch the characters and the setting, cut the images apart, paint the colours and build the setting, and finish the product (student book) | Book 145, printed p. 52 |
| Vietnam — Lesson 14 — designing a chair from foam and fabric materials, step by step — draw the product model according to the idea, choose and process the materials according to the product's details, build the frame part model and use the fabric to cover the product, and finish; the aesthetic tendency in the contemporary design field usually balances between simple single lines, flexibly, with straight lines and curved lines, limiting the use of many dots and complex details, and using used materials is the tendency in household design because this creates useful products and also contributes to saving resources and protecting the living environment (student book) | Book 145, printed p. 60 |

**Book 146:** *Mĩ thuật 10 — Kiến trúc (student book, Vietnamese)* — Title as printed: Mĩ thuật 10 — Kiến trúc (Fine Art 10 — Architecture; Kết nối tri thức với cuộc sống) — the Grade 10 specialist volume for the Architecture strand Grade: 10 (student/teacher: STUDENT) Publisher: Nhà xuất bản Giáo dục Việt Nam; year 2022; ISBN 978-604-0-31100-9 PDF pages: 34 (cover/title page verified against the record title) Verdict: COMPLETE Vietnam's own way: the specialist Architecture volume teaches architecture as the art of organising space — its characteristics (function, aesthetics, sustainability), its kinds by use, its values (use, aesthetic, cultural, historical) and its design process — read through Vietnamese works from the Cổ Loa citadel and the traditional rural house's ground plan to the One Pillar Pagoda, the Hanoi Opera House and the Nậm Đăm community house and homestay, whose swallow's-wing roof the pupils follow from brief to built work. | Vietnam — Lesson 2 (Did You Know) — the steps of showing the idea of an architectural work by a model (mô hình): Step 1: sketch the architectural work idea by drawing (phác thảo ý tưởng công trình kiến trúc bằng bản vẽ); Step 2: create the basic shape-mass for the model (tạo hình khối cơ bản cho mô hình); Step 3: perform the making (gia công) of the parts and details for the work such as main doors, windows, patterns (hoa văn)…; Note: choose the material and the showing material suitably with the character and characteristics of the work — for example, a work with many slender, light details (chi tiết thanh mảnh) and flat surface areas (mảng diện phẳng) can use the materials cardboard (bìa), paper and wood…; with a work with a solid mass (khối đặc) and many sculpture-style curved details (chi tiết cong kiểu điêu khắc), use the mass-creating materials such as foam (xốp), plaster (thạch cao) and clay (đất sét)…; Step 4: design the space around for the architectural model at a detailed level with the components such as water lake, trees, paths and rockery (hòn non bộ)…; and Apply: design the idea of the architectural work "Ngôi nhà mơ ước" (The Dream House) with a garden and show it by drawing and model, using the available materials and prioritising the recycled materials (reference: models of branches and dry leaves (mô hình cành cây, lá cây khô); a model in clay (mô hình bằng đất sét); a model in wood (mô hình bằng gỗ); a model in paper (mô hình bằng giấy); a model in cardboard (mô hình bằng bìa)) (student book) | Book 146, printed p. 24 |

**Book 147:** *Mĩ thuật 10 — Thiết kế công nghiệp / Industrial Design (student book, Vietnamese)* — Title as printed: Mĩ thuật 10 — Thiết kế công nghiệp (Kết nối tri thức với cuộc sống series) PDF pages: 34 (cover verified: printed title and grade match the record) Verdict: COMPLETE Vietnam's own way: industrial design is taught as a design branch of its own — product design, toy design and jewellery design — grounded in function and aesthetics, ergonomics and consumer psychology; toy design is practised from recycled and available materials, with safety rules for children stated outright Excluded (named, not placed): "Một số thuật ngữ dùng trong sách" (terms used in the book), printed p.31 — inventory only: use function (công năng sử dụng), designer (nhà thiết kế), aesthetics (tính thẩm mĩ), available material (vật liệu sẵn có), sketch drawing (vẽ phác thảo) Read in full in the 2026-11-16 Art expansion read. | Vietnam — Bài 2 Recognise — the steps of performing a children's toy product from available materials — find the idea → sketch and give the performing plan → choose and process the material suitable to the design → assemble the materials to create the product shape → complete the product → write the product description and use instructions; worked example: a preschool toy car combining bottle shells and plastic plates, taking the idea from car forms, sketched, built from cleaned used bottles, jars, straws and plastic caps, and finished with its written description — name "Siêu xe ngộ nghĩnh" (Funny Super Car), users preschool children 3–6 years, materials cleaned and pre-processed so they do not scratch skin or harm children, play by moving the car forwards–backwards and joining imaginary races, recommendation not for children under 3 (student book) | Book 147, printed p. 26 |
| Vietnam — Bài 2 Apply — pupil toy products made from available materials to give to poor children in the locality, following the steps (find the idea → sketch and give the plan → create details and assemble the product shape → coordinate colours and complete the decoration) — a ring-toss toy (đồ chơi ném đĩa) made from plastic plates, a fish-catching toy (đồ chơi câu cá) made from cardboard and ice-cream sticks, stilt toys (đồ chơi cà kheo) made from iron-can shells, and a table-football toy (đồ chơi đá bóng) made from a box shell and bamboo sticks (student book) | Book 147, printed p. 30 |

**Book 148:** *Chuyên đề học tập Mĩ thuật 11 / Art Study Topics 11 (student book, Vietnamese)* — Title as printed: Chuyên đề học tập Mĩ thuật 11 (Kết nối tri thức với cuộc sống series) PDF pages: 66 (cover verified: printed title and grade match the record) Verdict: COMPLETE Vietnam's own way: the Grade 11 art study topics are three deep practice topics — figure drawing through Vietnam's plaster plane-mass (phạt mảng) cast method, from the separate eye, nose, mouth and ear masses up to the whole portrait statue and its "golden proportions"; circle decoration, whose heritage examples are Vietnam's own (Trần-dynasty fired-earth tiles, Lý–Trần brown ceramics); and the figure composition painting, taught almost entirely through Vietnamese masters (Nguyễn Sáng, Tô Ngọc Vân, Nguyễn Phan Chánh, Trần Văn Cẩn, Nguyễn Tư Nghiêm) beside Delacroix, Gauguin, Matisse, Picasso and Velázquez Excluded (named, not placed): the three "Trưng bày sản phẩm" (product display) sections, printed pp.25, 45, 62 — display-and-assessment activities; "Giải thích thuật ngữ" (terms explanation), printed p.63 — inventory only: structure, material feeling, emphasis, rhythm, model, plane-cutting (phạt mảng), proportion, golden ratio, sketching Read in full in the 2026-11-16 Art expansion read. Grade 7 (STUDENT) Publisher: АОО «Назарбаев Интеллектуальные школы», Astana; 2018 (developed with the Institute of Education, University College London, per the NIS «Искусство» programme for grades 6–10, version 1, June 2016); ISBN 978-601-328-153-7 (Kazakh ed.), 978-601-328-156-8 (Russian ed.), 978-601-328-152-0 (English ed.) Authors: Ахметкалиева С.А. (visual art), Достанова У.К. (visual art), Акжереева К.Б. (music; named Элтерова in the Kazakh imprint), Кайдарова А.А. (music), Касенов Р.М. (music) PDF pages: 100 (Kazakh and Russian eds.), 92 (English ed.) = records MATCH (printed book 100 pp.) Records covered: targets-kz-b idx37 (Kazakh ed., read in full) + idx38 (Russian ed.) + idx39 (English ed.) — three parallel language editions of one book, placed once Verdict: COMPLETE Kazakhstan's own way: the NIS integrated «Art» subject alternates Music and Visual Art chapters in one book, and every world topic is anchored in Kazakh material: Kurmangazy's «Балбырауын» kui is compared in orchestral and rock-group performances, Abai's «Көзімнің қарасы» is sung and re-composed, Kasteev's portraits stand beside Vermeer and Van Gogh, the first Kazakh animated film (Amen Khaidarov's «Қарлығаштың құйрығы неге айыр?») opens the animation story, Kuat Shildebaev's film scores represent living Kazakh cinema music, and the «Астана Опера» theatre is the research object for theatre music. Named but not placed: chapter summaries (Қорытындылайық) — Music ch. 1, p. 16; Music ch. 2, p. 40; Music ch. 3, p. 58; Music ch. 4, p. 78; Visual Art ch. 1, p. 30; Visual Art ch. 2, p. 48; Visual Art ch. 3, p. 68; whole-book summary table, p. 88 — section summary lessons, per brief; Сөздік (trilingual Kazakh–Russian–English glossary), pp. 89–99 — inventory only, per brief. Read in the 2026-11-16 Art expansion read. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/kazakhstan-placements.md.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Kazakhstan — Бейнелеу өнері 1-тарау (continued). Қайта өңделген материалдардан портрет (A portrait from recycled materials) — what it teaches: the American artist Tom Denninger is called an ecologist artist, because he makes portraits from the things he uses in his pictures — he uses varied waste things: buttons of different colours, toys and toy parts, beads from necklaces, small tubes made of plastic and so on; Tom says his works draw most people's attention to environmental problems — «Орман, жағажай мен көшелердің қоқыс және темекі тұқылдарымен ластануы табиғат әлеміне қатты әсер етеді» ("The pollution of forests, beaches and streets with rubbish and cigarette ends strongly affects the world of nature"); the practical task: draw a portrait using the appliqué (аппликация) technique — for this print your friend's or family member's coloured photograph in A3 size; if there is no coloured photo, a black-and-white photo will also do; prepare pieces cut from journals, booklets, brochures or illustrations' pages; at work pay attention to these points: first look at the photograph's colours, then stick the pieces on it; this work's result must be seen fully; if needed, some places can be finished with a brush; PVA glue or any glue is right for sticking; dry the work well and do not hold the appliqué upside down for long; pieces of paper can be stuck not only by colour but also by texture — for example hair can be made with wavy (бұйра) paper; look at how the paper pieces' colours must match the photograph's colours and sizes: light and dark colours, warm and cold colours; all parts and the background must be stuck; Patrick Bremer's portraits made of newspaper and journal pieces, and Elliot Frantz's picture from a cardboard piece, pictured — cardboard (қыртысты қағаз) can also be used for appliqué; its wavy surface gives a special texture; points to note at work: for depicting hair a torn (жыртылған) cardboard texture is used; for sticking cardboard PVA glue is needed; the face's some parts can be drawn with paints mixed with a little water; on the cardboard's front, with a dark colour, volume can be given to the face's other parts with a pen (student book; original target `visual-art/collage`) | Book 173, printed p. 28 |

**Book 174:** *Өнер. 8 сынып / Искусство. Учебник для 8 класса* — Kazakhstan. Grade 8 (STUDENT) Publisher: АОО «Назарбаев Интеллектуальные школы», Nur-Sultan; 2019 (per the NIS «Искусство» programme for grades 6–10, version 2, May 2017); ISBN 978-601-328-324-1 / 978-601-328-336-4 (the two ISBNs printed across the two language editions) Authors: Ахметкалиева С.А., Ермилова Е.В., Пак Е.Л. PDF pages: 152 = records MATCH (printed book 152 pp.) Records covered: targets-kz-b idx46 (Kazakh ed., read in full) + idx47 (Russian ed., spot-checked identical — imprint and the batik lesson at the same printed p. 15) Verdict: COMPLETE Kazakhstan's own way: a visual-art-only NIS book in four sections — 2D microsystems, 3D microorganisms, product design and pop-art — and the Kazakhstani thread runs through all of it: Gulnar Umirbek's, Yulia Ismagbetova's and Tansyk Mazhitova's batik, the national game Togyzkumalak analysed as board-game design, and a whole Kazakhstani pop-art survey (Bernd Lutz's media-mix collages, Kuanysh Bazargaliev's «Қошқармүйіз» ram's-horn series, Zulkharnai Kozhamkulov's pop portraits, Saule Suleimenova's paintings on cellophane packets), ending in packaging design for Kazakh products (Ainash Ybyldaeva's «Құртафела» kurt). Named but not placed: section summaries (Қорытындылайық) — Section 1, p. 30; Section 2, p. 64; Section 3, p. 102; Section 4, p. 140 — section summary lessons, per brief; Соңғы сөз (final word), p. 141; Глоссарий, pp. 142–148 — inventory only, per brief. Read in the 2026-11-16 Art expansion read. Placements at liqlearns-courses-work/expansion-art-2026-11-16/kazakhstan-placements.md.

| Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Kazakhstan — 2.2 (continued). Ребекка Коулс және коллаж туралы жаңа деректер (Rebecca Coles and new data about collage) — what it teaches: read, interesting!: the British artist and sculptor Rebecca Coles (Ребекка Коулс) usually makes butterflies from thick papers; she calls it real magic (нағыз сиқыр); Rebecca Coles draws each butterfly's depiction separately, cuts it and even paints it in a special colour; look at the pictured works and think what idea this work conveys; truly, it amazes; research the pictures: the butterfly is a creative idea born from nature's form; butterflies' and moths' (көбелектер мен ...) wings' colour and form are varied; butterflies can be a symbol; to show this idea, what artistic methods can be used?; write this idea in the research album; new data about collage (коллаж туралы жаңа деректер): collage (коллаж) is an art work made by gluing elements of varied colour and texture from the main material onto a basis; a work made in the collage technique together with collage is also called so; collage can be finished by drawing with ink, watercolour and so on; the collage technique was first used in 1910–1912 by Georges Braque (Жорж Брак) and Pablo Picasso (Пабло Пикассо); do the practical work: take one of the previously drawn insects' parts' pictures and join them into one collage picture; in the work to be carried out beforehand, unite the creative idea, material and execution technique (student book; original target `visual-art/collage`) | Book 174, printed p. 35 |
| Kazakhstan — 4.4 Поп-арт стиліндегі суретшілер (Artists in the pop-art style) — what it teaches: in 1952 in London the «Тәуелсіз топ» (Independent Group) named pop-art artists' union opened; its artists R. Paolozzi and R. Hamilton led it; they are known to many: images (бейнелері) of known signs — goods' emblems and logotypes (тауар эмблемалары мен логотиптері), actors, singers, television presenters like known genres' images — through contemporary advertising-making technology and its effect on people's consciousness they researched; pop-art is an art propagating social equality (әлеуметтік теңдікті насихаттайтын өнер), because it aesthetically turns the public's consumed goods (тұтынатын тауарларын эстетикалық) into art (?); for example biscuit and chocolate packagings, known gas drinks' bottles, in one word people's social state regardless, the things used by many are used; analyse the pictures: the pop-art artist R. Hamilton's («Just what is it that makes today's homes so different, so appealing?» / «Кім біледі?» (Who knows?)) pictures' analysis: what is depicted in the picture?; what theme did the artist touch in his work?; what words are there in it?; what materials did the artist use?; in what technique is the work carried out?; write in the research album; Richard Hamilton (Ричард Гамильтон) is a British artist, pop-art's main founder (негізін қалаушы); his «Сонымен, бүгінгі үйлерімізді осындай әртүрлі әрі тартымды ететін не?» ("Just what is it that makes today's homes so different, so appealing?") named collage is considered one of the works drawn in the pop-art style for the first time; the collage composition made of cuttings from known journals in 1957 became famous; in his works artists using images taken directly from life began to be called «pop artists» (поп суретшілер) — «pop art» (поп-арт) definition was first given by Richard Hamilton, that is his letter: «Поп-арт — танымал (бұқаралық аудитория үшін жасалған), өткінші (қысқа мерзімді кезең үшін жасалған), бір реттік, арзан, көптеп өндірілген, жастық (жастарға бағытталған)...» ("Pop-art is popular (made for a mass audience), transient (made for a short period), expendable, cheap, mass-produced, youthful..."); youth-filled (жастарға толы) ... big business (үлкен бизнес); Eduardo Paolozzi (Эдуардо Паолоцци) — a Scottish graphic artist and sculptor, a known representative of British pop-art; he experimented with the silk-screen (жібекбаспа) technique and made many collages; the artist in his works used asymmetrical (асимметриялы) ornaments consisting of dots and lines, torn (жыртылған) or crumpled paper sheets; the practical work: entering British artists' works, make a collage in the pop-art style; first define the collage's theme; the collage can be not only a picture, but a poster (афиша) announcing a concert or another event can also be; prepare the materials and start carrying it out; Robert Indiana's (Роберт Индиана) words quoted: «Поп – бұл махаббат, себебі бәрі попты мойындайды... Поп бомбаның жарылысына ұқсас» ("Pop is love, because everyone admits pop... Pop is like a bomb's explosion") (his LOVE sculpture pictured) (student book; original target `visual-art/collage`) | Book 174, printed p. 110 |

**Book 175:** *Көркем еңбек. 8-сынып (ұл балаларға арналған нұсқа) / Художественный труд. 8 класс (вариант для мальчиков)* — Kazakhstan. Grade 8 (STUDENT) Publisher: «Келешек-2030» ЖШС, Kokshetau; 2018; ISBN 978-601-317-347-4 KZ / 978-601-317-341-2 RU Authors: Чукалин В.Г., Яковлев Р.М., Танбаев Х.К., Ермилова Е.В., Велькер Е.Е., Лосенко О.С. PDF pages: 80 = records MATCH (printed book 80 pp.) Records covered: targets-kz-b idx49 (Kazakh ed., read in full) + idx51 (Russian ed., spot-checked identical — imprint and the Togyzkumalak page at the same printed p. 43) Verdict: COMPLETE Kazakhstan's own way: mosaic is taught through the Khoja Akhmet Yassawi mausoleum and Kazakhstani mosaic portraits of Abai and Batyr Bauyrzhan; the design project is a Kazakh leather торсық and a round wooden table; and the board-game project opens with Togyzkumalak, the Kazakh national game of the sacred nine. Named but not placed: § 19 Агротехнологияның дамуындағы ғылымның рөлі (the role of science in agrotechnology development — self-directed research), pp. 60–61 — Home culture section, other-subject content; § 20 Электрқұрылғылар мен тұрмыстық техникаларды таңдау және бағалау (choosing and evaluating electrical devices and household appliances), p. 62 — Home culture section, other-subject content; § 21 Үй-жайды жөндеуге арналған заманауи материалдар мен құрал-жабдықтар (modern materials and tools for home repair — practical work), p. 64 — Home culture section, other-subject content; Санитарлық-гигиеналық талаптар (sanitary-hygienic requirements) and Қауіпсіздік ережелері (safety rules), pp. 68–73 — apparatus; Сөздік (glossary), pp. 74–77 — inventory only, per brief; Қосымша (appendix), p. 78 — the Kazakh ornament's main motifs table (zoomorphic, plant, cosmogonic, geometric families) — a reference inventory supporting §§ 9–10, not a lesson. | Lesson part | Where it comes from |
|---|---|
| Kazakhstan — § 1. Мозаика өнері (The art of mosaic) — what it teaches: mosaic (мозаика) is composing a picture or pattern from many uniform or varied materials; this decorative-applied art kind's history begins very early — mosaic art began to be used in the second half of the 4th millennium BC; mosaic is distinguished by its variety; in ancient times houses, palaces and temples were decorated with mosaic; when the Byzantine empire flourished, churches, mausoleums and temples were decorated with mosaic; mosaic art's notable samples are preserved in architectural monuments, chests, household items and furniture; the decorative mosaic and fragments of the Khoja Akhmet Yassawi mausoleum (Қ. А. Ясауи кесенесі) are pictured (student book; original target `visual-art/mosaic-art`) | Book 175, printed p. 8 |
| Kazakhstan — § 2. Мозаика орындаудың түрлері мен техникалары (Types and techniques of making mosaic) — what it teaches: mosaic is classified variously by the material used, the making technique, the mosaic pattern's kind and so on; by material — glass (шыны), ceramic (қыш), metal, smalt (смальта), stone (тас), wood (ағаш), and made of special (non-traditional) materials; mosaic's main element is the tessera (тессер) — the small pieces composing the whole composition; mosaic is composed by two methods — direct (оң) and reverse (теріс); in the direct method the mosaic is laid looking at the material's face side — the picture stands clear, at the moment of laying slanted places can be noticed at once and corrected, and this method is very convenient when laying uniform tesserae in rows; by the direct method mosaic can be set deep into a building's wall; in the reverse method the mosaic is laid face down, then solution is poured to fix the tesserae, and the finished picture is transferred to the material the mosaic falls on; in the reverse method how the mosaic fell can be seen only when everything is finished; this method is used when working with tesserae of varied thickness (student book; original target `visual-art/mosaic-art`) | Book 175, printed p. 10 |
| Kazakhstan — § 4. Мозаика панносына арналған негіз дайындау (Preparing the base for a mosaic panel) — what it teaches: a product's being beautiful depends not only on the material's correct choice but also on the base prepared for the mosaic panel; mosaic can be used not only as a finished product but, together with it, for decorating other things and souvenirs — for example a table's surface, a frame for putting a photograph and other things; the frequently used mosaic bases in the scheme — cement, stone, gypsum, concrete, cement-sand, plaster (сылақ), gypsum board, special; when preparing the base for a mosaic panel, first level it, if there are cracked places fill them; the face must be clean and smooth; then it must be painted out — then the base will set firm; transferring the sketch to the base — copy paper, tracing paper (калька) and a template can be used; a picture with a transparent film can also be glued (with the glue side up); the protective film must be taken off gradually, because the glue layer can separate in the air; then transfer the sketch to the base and fix its edges with sticky tape, and with a pencil or another sharp thing draw along the picture on the base so a trace stays; the lines fallen on the other face must be gone over with a marker or gel pen (student book; original target `visual-art/making-a-mosaic-panel`) | Book 175, printed p. 14 |
| Kazakhstan — § 5. Мозаика панносін орындау (Making the mosaic panel) — what it teaches: panel variant 1 in step photographs — a crow mosaic laid piece by piece from the drawing; prepare the materials and tools needed for composing mosaic beforehand; when breaking tile or ceramic board, wrap the shards in thick cloth so they do not fly; the mosaic pieces' sharp points can injure the hand — they can be ground with sandpaper; use a glass cutter (шыныкескіш) for the tesserae to be even; paint each part of the mosaic picture carefully; when it dries and darkens, the excess places can be wiped off with a damp cloth; panel variant 2 — the «Домбыра дауысы» (Voice of the Dombra) pebble panel: for the mosaic to come out as even and harmonious as possible, begin from the centre and gradually move to the edges; for the face layer add one trowel of cement to three or four trowels of sand; likewise, to show the seams clearly, to match the main colour or to show it separately, varied colour dry pigment can be added; in composing mosaic begin from the main pictures, then move to the surroundings; Zh. Rabat's author's mosaic works are pictured («Таңбалы тас», «Қабыршақ аралы», «Чихуахуа Сiao») (student book; original target `visual-art/making-a-mosaic-panel`) | Book 175, printed p. 16 |

Kazakhstan is added as student-book stock (see its rows above), inside the acceptance count; its teacher-material rows are evidence only, kept outside the acceptance count.
